The **Iraq national football team** ([Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): منتخب الْعِرَاق لِكُرَةُ الْقَدَم), nicknamed the **Lions of Mesopotamia** ([Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): أسود الرافدين),[^cite_note-umbrokit2021-2] represents [Iraq](7515928.md) in men's international [football](10568.md) and is controlled by the [Iraq Football Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_Football_Association) (IFA), the governing body for [football in Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_Iraq). Iraq's usual home venue is the [Basra International Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basra_International_Stadium).[^cite_note-3]

Iraq has qualified for the [FIFA World Cup](11370.md) on two occasions, in [1986](157231.md) and [2026](36581929.md), exiting at the group stage both times. Iraq scored its first World Cup goal against [Belgium](679716.md) in 1986 and added a second against [Norway](203277.md) in 2026. Iraq is one of eight [AFC](715249.md) nations to have won the [AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Asian_Cup), claiming the title in [2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_AFC_Asian_Cup) in spite of difficult conditions and limited preparation. Iraq defeated some of the favourites in the competition including [Australia](4792249.md), [South Korea](1018627.md) and [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_national_football_team). Iraq went on to earn two points at the [2009 FIFA Confederations Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_FIFA_Confederations_Cup) and finish fourth at the [2015 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_AFC_Asian_Cup).[^cite_note-4]

At the regional level, Iraq is the [Arab Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Arab_Cup)'s most successful nation with four titles and has also won the [Arabian Gulf Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabian_Gulf_Cup) four times and the [WAFF Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WAFF_Championship) once.[^cite_note-5] Iraq is known for its passionate football fans and the national team is seen as a symbol of hope and unity for Iraqi people.[^cite_note-6] The team reached its highest [FIFA World Ranking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Rankings) of 39th in October 2004.[^cite_note-7]

## History

Main article: [History of the Iraq national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Iraq_national_football_team)

### Early years and regional success (1948–1979)



The Iraqi national football team in 1951; they played friendly games in the Turkish cities of [İzmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0zmir) and [Ankara](802.md).

On 8 October 1948, the [Iraq Football Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_Football_Association) was founded.[^cite_note-Iraqi_Football_History-8] The Iraq FA joined [FIFA](11049.md) in 1950 and in April 1951, Iraq played their first match: a 5–0 win over the Civil Cantonment (C.C.) team of [Habbaniyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habbaniyah).[^cite_note-9][^cite_note-Iraqi_Football_History-8] Iraq's first ever official international game came in the opening game of the [1957 Arab Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_1957_Arab_Games) in [Beirut](37428.md) where Iraq drew 3–3 to [Morocco](1024865.md) with goals from [Ammo Baba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ammo_Baba), [Youra Eshaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youra_Eshaya) (both from Iraq's [Assyrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyrian_people) minority) and [Fakhri Mohammed Salman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fakhri_Mohammed_Salman).[^cite_note-10][^cite_note-Iraqi_Football_History-8] One of the members of Iraq's first national team was [Youra Eshaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youra_Eshaya), who in 1954 became the first Iraqi footballer to play in England for [Bristol Rovers Colts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bristol_Rovers).[^cite_note-11]

In 1962, Iraq appointed their first foreign manager, [Romanian](25445.md) coach [Cornel Drăgușin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cornel_Dr%C4%83gu%C8%99in). Iraq won their first trophy in [1964](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964_Arab_Cup) when they won the [Arab Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Cup), winning three and drawing one of their four games. In [the following edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1966_Arab_Cup), they retained their Arab Cup title, beating [Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syria_national_football_team) 2–1 in the final in [Baghdad](4492.md).[^cite_note-Iraqi_Football_History-8]

In [1972](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_AFC_Asian_Cup), Iraq played at their first ever [AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Asian_Cup) but failed to win a game in the tournament. In March 1973, Iraq played their first ever [FIFA World Cup](11370.md) qualifying campaign. They finished second in their [group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(AFC_and_OFC\)#Group_2_2), a point behind [Australia](4792249.md), therefore failing to qualify for the next round. In the remaining years of the 1970s, Iraq reached the second round of the [Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_Asian_Games) ([1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_1974_Asian_Games)), lost the [Arabian Gulf Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_Cup_of_Nations) final ([1976](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations)), finished fourth at the AFC Asian Cup ([1976](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_AFC_Asian_Cup)), finished fourth in the Asian Games ([1978](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_1978_Asian_Games)) and finally hosted and won the Arabian Gulf Cup ([1979](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1979_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations)).[^cite_note-Trophies-12] The 1976 Asian Cup would be the last Asian Cup that Iraq appeared in for the next 20 years, as they withdrew from the next three editions.

### 1980s – First Golden Generation

The 1980s was arguably Iraq's most successful period in their history. They started the decade off disappointingly, being knocked out in the first round of [qualifiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification) for the [1982 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_FIFA_World_Cup). In 1982, they won the gold medal at the [1982 Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_1982_Asian_Games). In 1984, Iraq won the [Arabian Gulf Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations). The following year, they won the [1985 Arab Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1985_Arab_Cup) and also won the gold medal at the [1985 Arab Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_1985_Arab_Games).[^cite_note-13]

#### 1986 FIFA World Cup: Iraq’s historic first qualification

Iraq were seeded into the [first round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1986_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(AFC\)#First_round) of qualifiers where they faced [Qatar](1111350.md) and [Jordan](1090065.md). Iraq topped Group 1B with 6 points, and advanced to the second round. Iraq faced United Arab Emirates in two legs. Iraq defeated UAE 3–2 in [Dubai](211583.md). Iraq lost 2–1 to UAE in the second leg. Iraq progressed on away goals after a 4–4 aggregate scoreline and advanced to the final round. In the final round, Iraq drew 0–0 against Syria in [Damascus](8914.md).[^cite_note-14] Iraq defeated Syria 3–1 in the second leg in [Taif](201969.md). Iraq won 3–1 on aggregate and qualified for the [1986 FIFA World Cup](157231.md).[^cite_note-15]

At their first game of the Group B at the [1986 FIFA World Cup](157231.md), Iraq played well against [Paraguay](804025.md), losing narrowly 1–0 despite scoring a goal that was controversially disallowed by the referee. Iraq recorded their first World Cup goal in the second game, scoring against [Belgium](679716.md) in a 1–2 defeat despite having ten men, with [Ahmed Radhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_Radhi) scoring a goal for Iraq.[^cite_note-16] Iraq played against hosts [Mexico](808402.md) in the third game, losing 1–0 and being eliminated from the World Cup.[^cite_note-17]

In the following years, Iraq won the [1988 Arabian Gulf Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations) and won the [1988 Arab Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Arab_Cup). Overall, Iraq won nine competitions in the 1980s and played in their first World Cup, leading many to believe that this was the golden era of Iraqi football. In 1989, Iraq competed in qualifying for a berth in the 1990 World Cup finals, but they lost a crucial game against Qatar.[^cite_note-18]

### 1990s – The Dark Era

Following the [Gulf War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War) in 1990, Iraq was banned from participating in the [Asian Games](39205.md) and in most Arab competitions. The ban wasn't extended to the World Cup.

#### 1994 FIFA World Cup qualification: The Agony of Doha

In 1993, Iraq participated in qualifiers for the [1994 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup). Grouped with [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_national_football_team), [Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yemen_national_football_team), [Jordan](1090065.md) and [Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_national_football_team) in the first stage, Iraq proved to be too strong for the rest, with the team only failing to win twice, a draw to Jordan and loss to China.

In the final round, Iraq was grouped with [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_national_football_team), [South Korea](1018627.md), [Japan](993546.md), [Iran](272865.md) and [North Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Korea_national_football_team). On the first matchday, Iraq lost 3–2 to [North Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Korea_national_football_team) in a dramatic way, being 2–0 ahead in the second half. After an Iraqi player was sent off, North Korea pulled back in the 67th minute, equalized in the 77th minute and scored the winner in the 82nd minute.[^cite_note-19] Iraq then tied 2–2 with [South Korea](1018627.md).[^cite_note-20] and then defeated [Iran](272865.md) 2–1 by goals of [Ahmed Radhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_Radhi) and [Alaa Kadhim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaa_Kadhim).[^cite_note-21]

Iraq missed out on a World Cup spot by two points. By drawing their last game with [Japan](993546.md) 2–2, they denied the Japanese a place in the finals in a match referred to by the Japanese media as the [Agony of Doha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agony_of_Doha).[^cite_note-Agony_of_Doha-22] The loss against North Korea proved to be the difference with the other teams, as North Korea lost their other 4 matches and finished bottom of the group.

#### 1996 AFC Asian Cup: Return after two decades

Iraq participated in the [1996 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_AFC_Asian_Cup), their first Asian Cup campaign for 20 years after being banned in [1992](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_AFC_Asian_Cup) and withdrawing from the three tournaments before that. They reached the quarter-finals but lost to the United Arab Emirates due to a [golden goal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_goal) scored by [Abdulrahman Ibrahim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdulrahman_Ibrahim). In 1996, Iraq was ranked 139th in the world, which is their worst FIFA ranking in their history due to inactivity after withdrawing from several tournaments.

In 1997, Iraq participated in qualifiers for the [1998 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_FIFA_World_Cup) but were knocked out at the [first round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_first_round) following two defeats by [Kazakhstan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazakhstan_national_football_team).

This period is known as 'The Dark Era' as [Uday Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uday_Hussein), the son of [Saddam Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam_Hussein) and then-president of IFA, abused his control of Iraqi football and tortured players who played poorly, punishing them by sending them to prison, making them bathe in raw sewage and kick concrete balls, and shaving their heads among many other punishments.[^cite_note-Uday-23][^cite_note-24]

### 2000s – Second Golden Generation

The 2000s was considered to be the rebirth of a new Iraqi football golden generation. However, Iraq had a rocky beginning. Iraq played in the [2000 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_AFC_Asian_Cup) but were knocked out at the quarter-final stage again, this time by Japan in a 4–1 loss.[^cite_note-25] Iraq reached the [final round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_second_round) of [2002 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIFA_World_Cup) qualification for the first time since [1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification) but lost five of their eight second-round games and therefore failed to make the finals.[^cite_note-26] Iraq won their first ever WAFF Championship in [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_West_Asian_Football_Federation_Championship), beating Jordan 3–2 in the final after extra time.[^cite_note-27]

In the [2004 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_AFC_Asian_Cup), Iraq lost the opening match against [Uzbekistan](1032413.md), then they won against [Turkmenistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkmenistan_national_football_team) and [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_national_football_team) respectively, to reach the quarter-finals before getting knocked out by China.[^cite_note-28] In the same year they were knocked out at the [second round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_second_round) of [2006 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_FIFA_World_Cup) qualifiers by Uzbekistan.[^cite_note-29]

Iraq were ranked as high as 39th in the World Rankings in October 2004 which is their highest ranking position in their history. The following year, Iraq won the gold medal in the [West Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Asian_Games) by beating Syria in the final via a penalty shootout. In 2007, Iraq were knocked out at the group stage of the [Arabian Gulf Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations) after a controversial defeat to [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_national_football_team).[^cite_note-Gulf_Cup_2007-30] Akram Salman was sacked and [Jorvan Vieira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorvan_Vieira) appointed as head coach. Under him, Iraq reached the final of the [WAFF Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_West_Asian_Football_Federation_Championship) but lost 2–1 to Iran.[^cite_note-31]

#### 2007 AFC Asian Cup: Iraq's famous first continental title



Iraq playing against [Australia](4792249.md) in [Group A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_AFC_Asian_Cup_Group_A) of the [2007 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_AFC_Asian_Cup); Iraq won the game 3–1 on their way to winning the cup.

In July 2007, Iraq kicked off their [2007 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_AFC_Asian_Cup) campaign. The squad was made mainly of players that won the [2000 AFC Youth Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_AFC_Youth_Championship), finished fourth at the [2004 Olympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_2004_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men's_tournament) and second at the [2006 Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_2006_Asian_Games). Vieira only had two months to prepare his team for the tournament, and the team suffered from very poor facilities. The Iraq FA struggled to provide the team with enough kits for the tournament and Iraq had not been able to play any previous games in their own country for security reasons and most of the players had had family members killed in the war.[^cite_note-\:0-32]

The team started the tournament with a 1–1 draw against joint-hosts [Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thailand_national_football_team) before producing a 3–1 win over favourites Australia. A draw with [Oman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oman_national_football_team) followed to put Iraq into the quarter-finals where two goals from [Younis Mahmoud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Younis_Mahmoud) against [Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_national_football_team) put Iraq into the semi-finals for the second time in their history. They managed to knock out one of the best Asian teams, [South Korea](1018627.md) in the semis via a penalty shootout in which Noor Sabri made a crucial save.[^cite_note-\:0-32] After the game, a suicide bomber killed 30 football fans who were celebrating the semi-final win over South Korea and this almost led to the Iraqi team withdrawing from the final,[^cite_note-33] but they decided to go on in honour of the dead and succeeded in doing that after defeating Saudi Arabia 1–0 in the final, a game that they dominated from start to finish and that was won by a Younis Mahmoud header. This tournament win is seen as one of the greatest upsets in international history as a war-torn country became international champions in what is described as one of sport's greatest 'fairytales'.[^cite_note-34][^cite_note-35]

Vieira stated during the final that he would resign after the Asian Cup.[^cite_note-36] He was replaced by [Egil Olsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egil_Olsen) in September 2007.[^cite_note-37]

#### 2010 FIFA World Cup qualification: Controversial early exit

Under [Egil Olsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egil_Olsen), Iraq advanced from the first round by beating [Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_national_football_team) 7–0 on aggregate. Then they were drawn with [Australia](4792249.md), [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_national_football_team), and [Qatar](1111350.md) for the second round.[^cite_note-38] On the first matchday, Iraq tied China. As a consequence, [Olsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egil_Olsen) was sacked and replaced by [Adnan Hamad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adnan_Hamad).[^cite_note-39] The next two matches saw Iraq lose twice to [Qatar](1111350.md) and [Australia](4792249.md). However, Iraq bounced back with a 1–0 win over Australia and 2–1 win against China.[^cite_note-40]

On 26 May 2008, FIFA decided to suspend Iraq from all international competitions after the [Iraq Football Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_Football_Association) was disbanded by the government on 20 May 2008.[^cite_note-41] The suspension was provisionally and conditionally lifted on 29 May 2008.[^cite_note-42] Iraq needed 1 point to advance to the final stage on the last matchday, but failed to advance to the final round of [2010 FIFA World Cup](168079.md) qualifiers as a 1–0 defeat to [Qatar](1111350.md) saw them finish in third in the group. Following this, the Iraq FA decided to disband the team and sacked Hamad.[^cite_note-43]

Qatar fielded ineligible player [Emerson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%A1rcio_Emerson_Passos) in the 2–0 defeat to Iraq on 26 March 2008, prompting FIFA to controversially[^cite_note-44] suspend him but clear Qatar of any wrongdoing, in direct contradiction to its decisions against [Singapore](#Group_4). Iraq appealed the decision to the [Court of Arbitration for Sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Arbitration_for_Sport) but it was rejected by the CAS, saying that Iraq submitted documents and appeal fees too late.[^cite_note-45]

Jorvan Vieira was reappointed in September 2008. After a disappointing [2009 Arabian Gulf Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations), Vieira was sacked and replaced by [Bora Milutinovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bora_Milutinovic).[^cite_note-46]

#### 2009 FIFA Confederations Cup: Iraq on the global stage

In 2009, Iraq participated in only their second FIFA tournament ever: the [2009 FIFA Confederations Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_FIFA_Confederations_Cup), which they qualified for by winning the 2007 AFC Asian Cup. They started the tournament with a 0–0 draw with hosts [South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_national_football_team), before losing 1–0 to [UEFA Euro 2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2008) winners [Spain](609825.md). Iraq drew the last game 0–0 with [New Zealand](1023035.md) and were knocked out.

On 20 November 2009, the FIFA Emergency Committee suspended the Iraq FA due to government interference;[^cite_note-47] the suspension was lifted on 19 March 2010.[^cite_note-48]

### 2010s – Ups and downs



The Iraqi national team pose ahead of their [2014 FIFA World Cup qualification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification) match against [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_national_football_team) in [Doha](26214389.md).

Iraq qualified automatically for the [2011 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_AFC_Asian_Cup), where they were drawn with Iran, North Korea and UAE. After losing 2–1 to Iran and beating UAE 1–0, Iraq defeated North Korea 1–0 to advance to the quarterfinals. They lost 1–0 to Australia after extra time, with [Harry Kewell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_Kewell) scoring in the 117th minute.[^cite_note-49]

Under [Wolfgang Sidka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolfgang_Sidka), Iraq entered the [2014 FIFA World Cup qualifiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_second_round), defeating [Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yemen_national_football_team) 2–0 and drawing 0–0 to advance. Sidka left in August 2011 and was replaced by Brazilian legend [Zico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zico_\(footballer\)).[^cite_note-50]

Iraq topped their third-round group with 15 points, but struggled in the final round against Jordan, Japan, Australia and Oman. After two opening draws, Iraq lost to Japan and Australia before beating Jordan 1–0 in Doha.[^cite_note-51] Zico resigned in November 2012 and was replaced by Vladimir Petrović in February 2013. Iraq were eliminated after losing 1–0 to Japan on 11 June 2013.[^cite_note-52]

#### 2015 AFC Asian Cup: Iraq's run to the semi-finals

[Iraq](7515928.md) were in a [2015 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_AFC_Asian_Cup) qualifying group alongside [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_national_football_team), [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_national_football_team) and [Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia_national_football_team). After losing consecutively against China and Saudi Arabia, Iraq was in danger of missing out on the Asian Cup. They kept alive their hopes of qualifying for the 2015 AFC Asian Cup with a 2–0 away win over Indonesia on 19 November 2013, and on the last matchday, Iraq booked their spot with a commanding 3–1 win at the Sharjah Stadium against China. Due to bad results at the [22nd Arabian Gulf Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/22nd_Arabian_Gulf_Cup), [Hakeem Shaker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakeem_Shaker) was sacked and the FA appointed [Radhi Shenaishil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radhi_Shenaishil) as caretaker coach.

Iraq began the [2015 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_AFC_Asian_Cup) campaign with a 1–0 win over [Jordan](1090065.md).[^cite_note-53] In the next match, Iraq faced [Japan](993546.md) and lost the match 0–1.[^cite_note-54] Iraq then beat [Palestine](1302455.md) 2–0 and qualified to knockout stage as the Group D runner-up.[^cite_note-55] Iraq defeated Iran in the quarter-finals in penalties, 7–6, after the game ended 3–3 after 120 minutes of play.[^cite_note-56] They faced [South Korea](1018627.md) in the semi-finals but lost 0–2.[^cite_note-57] Iraq finished the AFC Asian Cup in fourth place, after losing 2–3 to [United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Arab_Emirates_national_football_team) in third/fourth place play-off.[^cite_note-58]

#### 2018 FIFA World Cup qualification: Struggles in the final round

For the [2018 FIFA World Cup qualifiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_second_round), Iraq were drawn with [Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thailand_national_football_team), [Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_national_football_team) and [Chinese Taipei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Taipei_national_football_team).[^cite_note-59]

In August 2015, [Yahya Alwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahya_Alwan) was appointed coach for the [2018 FIFA World Cup qualifiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_second_round).[^cite_note-60] After initially starting well with a 5–1 win against [Chinese Taipei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Taipei_national_football_team), two consecutive draws against [Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thailand_national_football_team) and [Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_national_football_team) left Iraq in real danger of missing out on the final round. On 24 March 2016, Iraq tied 2–2 with [Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thailand_national_football_team) and Alwan was replaced with [Abdul-Ghani Shahad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul-Ghani_Shahad). Five days later, he led Iraq to the final round and [2019 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_AFC_Asian_Cup) by beating [Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_national_football_team) 1–0.[^cite_note-61]

On 15 April 2016, [Radhi Shenaishil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radhi_Shenaishil) became the new coach of Iraq in order to lead the team in the [final round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_third_round).[^cite_note-62] For the final round, Iraq was drawn with [Japan](993546.md), [Australia](4792249.md), [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_national_football_team), [UAE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Arab_Emirates_national_football_team), and [Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thailand_national_football_team). After seven matchdays, Iraq only managed to collect four points by beating [Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thailand_national_football_team) 4–0 and drawing 1–1 with [Australia](4792249.md). As a result, [Shenaishil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radhi_Shenaishil) was sacked on April 10, 2017.[^cite_note-63]

[Basim Qasim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basim_Qasim) was appointed for the remainder of the qualifiers and Iraq finished fifth in their group in the [final round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_third_round) of the [2018 FIFA World Cup qualification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification).

#### 2019 AFC Asian Cup: Defeat to the eventual champions



The Iraqi national team pose ahead of their [2019 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_AFC_Asian_Cup) match against [Iran](272865.md) in [Dubai](211583.md).

The [2019 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_AFC_Asian_Cup) draw put Iraq in Group D, with [Iran](272865.md), [Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam_national_football_team), and [Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yemen_national_football_team).[^cite_note-SWAFCqual19-64]

On 3 September 2018, [Srečko Katanec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sre%C4%8Dko_Katanec) was appointed as head coach on a three-year contract to lead Iraq in the [2019 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_AFC_Asian_Cup) and [2022 FIFA World Cup qualification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(AFC\)).[^cite_note-65]

Their first match took place on 8 January 2019 against Vietnam and ended in a 3–2 victory for Iraq.[^cite_note-66] Their second match against Yemen on January 12 ended in a 3–0 victory, with goals coming from Mohanad Ali, Bashar Resan, and Alaa Abbas. In their final group game, Iraq faced Iran on January 16, resulting in a 0–0 draw. With these performances, Iraq finished second in Group D and advanced to the knockout stage.[^cite_note-67]

In the Round of 16, Iraq faced Qatar. Despite their efforts, Iraq suffered a 1–0 defeat, with [Bassam Al-Rawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bassam_Al-Rawi) scoring the only goal, ending their journey in the tournament. [Qatar](1111350.md) later won the tournament.[^cite_note-68]

### 2020s – Recent history

Iraq participated in the second round of the Asian qualifiers for the [2022 FIFA World Cup](17742072.md). They were placed in Group C alongside Bahrain, Iran, Cambodia, and Hong Kong and reached the final round with five wins from eight matches including a 2–1 victory against [Iran](272865.md).[^cite_note-69]

Iraq went 19 consecutive matches without losing between 2019 and 2021 and moved up from 89th to 68th in the FIFA rankings during Katanec's tenure. Katanec departed in July 2021 after six months of unpaid wages and filed a complaint with [FIFA](11049.md).[^cite_note-70]

On 31 July 2021, Dutchman [Dick Advocaat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Advocaat) was appointed head coach of Iraq.[^cite_note-71] Under Advocaat, Iraq made to a slow start to the final round of World Cup Qualifiers, drawing four games and losing two, and on 21 November 2021, Advocaat resigned. [Željko Petrović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BDeljko_Petrovi%C4%87) took charge of the team for the [2021 FIFA Arab Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_FIFA_Arab_Cup), where Iraq were eliminated from the group stage. Petrović was sacked after two further winless qualifying games and [Abdul-Ghani Shahad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul-Ghani_Shahad) was appointed as an interim manager, but Iraq were eliminated after finishing fourth in the group.

On 7 November 2022, [Jesús Casas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jes%C3%BAs_Casas) was appointed head coach of the Iraq national football team, with a contract running until the [2026 FIFA World Cup](36581929.md).[^cite_note-72] In his first tournament in charge, Iraq hosted and won the [25th Arabian Gulf Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/25th_Arabian_Gulf_Cup), defeating [Oman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oman_national_football_team) 3–2 after extra time in the final.[^cite_note-73]



[Amir Al-Ammari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amir_Al-Ammari) challenges for the ball in a [2023 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_AFC_Asian_Cup) match which Iraq won 3–1 against [Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia_national_football_team).

Iraq was drawn in Group D of the [2023 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_AFC_Asian_Cup) with Japan, Vietnam, and Indonesia.[^cite_note-74] On 15 January 2024, Iraq overcame Indonesia 3–1.[^cite_note-75] Four days later, Iraq created the biggest shock in the tournament, stunning Japan (who had been undefeated in the last 11 matches) by beating them 2–1 in the second group match to ensure first place in the group; both Iraq goals were scored by [Aymen Hussein](47703032.md).[^cite_note-76] With the outcome confirmed, Iraq largely rotated their squad on the final match against Vietnam, and ended up triumphant 3–2 to make history by earning a perfect record of nine points.[^cite_note-77] However, Iraq suffered a shock defeat in the Round of 16, losing 2–3 to Jordan to exit the tournament. The result was controversial due to a second yellow card shown to Aymen Hussein by the referee for his celebration after he had scored to take a 2–1 lead.[^cite_note-78]

#### 2026 FIFA World Cup: Return to the World Cup after forty years

During the [second round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_second_round) of World Cup qualification, Iraq was placed in Group F alongside Vietnam, the Philippines, and Indonesia. The team had an impressive run, winning all six matches, securing qualification to the next round and for the [2027 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2027_AFC_Asian_Cup).[^cite_note-79] In the [third round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_third_round) of qualification, Iraq was drawn into Group B, which included South Korea, Jordan, Palestine, Oman, and Kuwait. However, despite early momentum, Iraq struggled to maintain consistency. Following a 2–1 defeat to Palestine on 27 March 2025, the [Iraq Football Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_Football_Association) (IFA) held an urgent meeting and unanimously decided to dismiss Casas and his coaching staff. His contract was officially terminated on 15 April 2025.[^cite_note-80]

Casas was succeeded by Australian coach [Graham Arnold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graham_Arnold), who previously coached [Australia](4792249.md) in the [2022 FIFA World Cup](17742072.md). Arnold took over for the final matches of the third round, but Iraq finished third in the group and missed out on direct qualification to the World Cup, instead proceeding to the fourth round of qualifiers. In the [fourth round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_fourth_round), Iraq were drawn with hosts Saudi Arabia and Indonesia. Iraq ended Indonesia's hope of qualification with a 1–0 victory,[^cite_note-81] however Iraq finished as group runners-up after a goalless draw with Saudi Arabia, who qualified to the World Cup on goals scored.[^cite_note-82] Iraq advanced to the two-legged [fifth round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_fifth_round) playoff against the United Arab Emirates; in the first leg in Abu Dhabi, the teams drew 1–1.[^cite_note-83] In the return leg in Basra, Iraq triumphed 2–1, winning the tie 3–2 on aggregate, thanks to a dramatic stoppage-time penalty converted by [Amir Al-Ammari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amir_Al-Ammari). With that victory, Iraq advanced to the [inter-confederation play-offs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(inter-confederation_play-offs\)),[^cite_note-84] where they were drawn to face the winner of the game between [Bolivia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolivia_national_football_team) and [Suriname](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suriname_national_football_team) in a play-off match held in Mexico to qualify for the 2026 FIFA World Cup.[^cite_note-85] Iraq ultimately faced Bolivia and defeated them 2–1, with [Aymen Hussein](47703032.md) scoring the decisive goal to secure Iraq's place at the World Cup.[^cite_note-86] Iraq's successful qualifying campaign for the 2026 FIFA World Cup spanned 21 matches over a period of 28 months.[^cite_note-87][^cite_note-88]

At the [2026 FIFA World Cup](36581929.md), Iraq was drawn into [Group I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_I) alongside [France](250197.md), [Norway](203277.md) and [Senegal](1017436.md). Iraq opened its campaign with a 4–1 defeat to Norway, despite [Aymen Hussein](47703032.md) scoring Iraq's first World Cup goal since 1986 with a headed equaliser, followed by a 3–0 defeat to France. Two costly defensive errors proved decisive in both games, while Iraq's tournament ended with a 5–0 loss to Senegal after receiving an early red card in the match. The Lions of Mesopotamia finished bottom of the group without a point, although their return to the World Cup after a 40-year absence was widely regarded as an important milestone in the national team's development.[^cite_note-89]

## Team image

### Colours

The traditional colours of the Iraq national football team are green, white and black. Green and white are often interchangeable as Iraq's home and away colours, while black is used as the third colour.

The national team frequently wore white kits during the 1960s and early 1970s, including at the [1966 Arab Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1966_Arab_Cup) and during early FIFA World Cup qualifying campaigns. Green became increasingly prominent from the mid-1970s onward, notably during the [4th Arabian Gulf Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4th_Arabian_Gulf_Cup) and the [1976 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_AFC_Asian_Cup). Over time, it developed into the team's primary football colour and was often combined with white details inspired by the national flag. The shade of green has varied across different periods, ranging from brighter tones in earlier decades to darker modern interpretations introduced by manufacturers such as [Adidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas) and [Umbro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umbro).[^cite_note-90]

Iraq have also previously worn red, yellow and blue kits. One of the most notable departures from the traditional colour scheme occurred during the [1986 FIFA World Cup](157231.md), when Iraq wore yellow against Paraguay and blue against Belgium and Mexico despite having mainly used green and white during qualification.[^cite_note-91] Former Iraqi players later stated that members of the team delegation had attempted to retain the traditional colours, but the request was rejected by then-Iraq Football Association president [Uday Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uday_Hussein), who reportedly insisted on using yellow and blue kits.[^cite_note-92] Following the tournament, Iraq gradually returned to green-and-white combinations.

Since the 2000s, green and white have again formed the basis of Iraq's visual identity, while black has increasingly been used for alternative kits, particularly in designs incorporating references to [Mesopotamian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesopotamia) civilisation, Babylonian ornamentation and traditional Iraqi motifs. One of the most notable examples was [Umbro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umbro)’s black third kit introduced in November 2021 for the [2021 FIFA Arab Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_FIFA_Arab_Cup), which featured geometric patterns inspired by Mesopotamian art, including Babylonian palm motifs symbolising victory, ornamental [Assyrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyria) flowers and decorative elements referencing the walls of the [Ishtar Gate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ishtar_Gate). The shirt also incorporated the word “Iraq” in [Kufic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kufic) script on the back and later received international attention among football shirt collectors, including a nomination by [Classic Football Shirts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classic_Football_Shirts) among the best football shirts of the year.[^cite_note-93]

### Kits

Wikimedia Commons logo

Wikimedia Commons has media related to [Iraq national football team kits](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Iraq%20national%20football%20team%20kits).

Iraq's kits have been manufactured by several international and regional sportswear brands, including [Umbro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umbro), [Adidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas), [Puma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puma_AG), [Jako](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jako), [Peak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peak_Sport_Products), [Givova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Givova), [Lotto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lotto_Sport_Italia), [Diadora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diadora) and [Jack & Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bestseller_\(company\)). The current kit supplier is [Jako](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jako).[^cite_note-94]

### Nickname



Lion in ceramic tile from the [Ishtar Gate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ishtar_Gate) in Babylon

The Iraqi team is commonly known as *Usood al-Rafidayn* ([Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): أُسُودُ الرَّافِدَيْن), meaning *"Lions of [Mesopotamia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesopotamia)"*. In [ancient Mesopotamia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Iraq), the [Babylonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babylonia) lion was a symbol of power, impetuosity, ferocity, prestige and dominance.[^cite_note-99] This is reflected in the sculpted lions in [Babylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babylon), where the [processional path](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ishtar_Gate) is ornamented with [ceramic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceramic) tile [bas-reliefs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Relief) representing a prestigious lion from the time of [Nebuchadnezzar II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nebuchadnezzar_II). This kind of representation aimed to glorify the king, master of the beasts, and also represent the defeat of the enemy.[^cite_note-100] Moreover, the Mesopotamian royal inscriptions depict the king as a ferocious lion to whom nothing can be resisted.[^cite_note-101][^cite_note-102][^cite_note-103] The [presence of lions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_depictions_of_lions) in ancient Iraqi civilization was based on the belief, or desire, that the animals represented would bring with them the virtues they symbolized, so that they could be transmitted to the owners.[^cite_note-104]

### Team logo

Iraq kits throughout history have usually featured the [flag of Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Iraq) on them, although the [coat of arms of Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Iraq) and the [Iraq Football Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_Football_Association) logo have both appeared on kits in the past. The national team has occasionally had its own unique logo, the first of which was from 1982 to 1983. This logo was based on the Iraq flag, with *Iraqi* written at the top of the crest.[^cite_note-WC_1986-105] From 2000 to 2002, the national team's logo featured a green outline with the word *Iraq* written at the top in green Arabic text. In the [2005 West Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_2005_West_Asian_Games), the team wore a new logo with the red band of the flag appearing in a large semi-circle shape,[^cite_note-WAG_2005-106] and in 2007, Iraq briefly reverted to using the logo that they had used from 2000 to 2002. On 23 October 2020, the national team's current logo was revealed. A star featured above the crest on the national team's shirts from 2021 to 2022 to commemorate the nation's [2007 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_AFC_Asian_Cup) victory.[^cite_note-107]

### Rivalries

Main articles: [Iran–Iraq football rivalry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Iraq_football_rivalry), [Iraq–Saudi Arabia football rivalry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq%E2%80%93Saudi_Arabia_football_rivalry), and [Iraq–Kuwait football rivalry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq%E2%80%93Kuwait_football_rivalry)

Due to its geographical location, Iraq maintains strong rivalries with many neighbours.

| Opponent | GP | W | D | L | GF | GA | GD | Win % |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [Iran](272865.md) | 31 | 6 | 7 | 18 | 21 | 43 | −22 | 019.35 |
| [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_national_football_team) | 45 | 18 | 12 | 15 | 63 | 45 | +18 | 040.00 |
| [Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_national_football_team) | 44 | 19 | 13 | 12 | 59 | 48 | +11 | 043.18 |

Iraq's main and traditional rival has been [Iran](272865.md), and they are often considered to be two of the greatest football teams in the Middle East and Asia with one of the greatest rivalries. At the early stage, Iran had proved to be more dominant than Iraq, remaining undefeated from 1964 until 1993. In the contemporary era, especially during the reign of [Saddam Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam_Hussein), the two countries had bad relations and fought the [Iran–Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Iraq_War) for eight years.[^cite_note-CNN-108][^cite_note-Iran_Daily-109] Iraqis have considered any matches against Iran as a must-win encounter and are known to treat it differently from any other football matches.[^cite_note-110] Iraq has played [31 matches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Iraq_football_rivalry) against Iran with [6 victories, 7 draws, and 18 losses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Iraq_football_rivalry).

Iraq's other rival is [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_national_football_team), and matches between the two teams also draw significant attention from Iraqi fans, with Iraq and Saudi Arabia being recognised as the two most successful Arab teams in Asia. The beginnings of the footballing rivalry between them dates back to the 1970s, but it was only after the 1990s that the rivalry between the two Arab nations truly developed since it was previously overshadowed by Iraq's rivalries with Iran and Kuwait.[^cite_note-111] One of these reasons for the rivalry to develop is due to the bitter [Gulf War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War), where Iraq fought against Saudi Arabia over [Kuwait](7515890.md), an ally of Saudi Arabia.[^cite_note-biz-112] These encounters have also been marred with various controversies and hostilities, such as the [21st Arabian Gulf Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/21st_Arabian_Gulf_Cup) hosting rights, where Iraq was stripped from hosting with the tournament instead being moved to Bahrain, a move which was believed by Iraqis as a deliberate act by Saudi Arabia to remove Iraq's home advantage.[^cite_note-biz-112] Before that, Iraq was also banned from hosting home games against Saudi Arabia due to the Gulf War.[^cite_note-biz-112] Iraq has played [45 matches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq%E2%80%93Saudi_Arabia_football_rivalry) against Saudi Arabia with [18 victories, 12 draws, and 15 losses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq%E2%80%93Saudi_Arabia_football_rivalry).

Iraq's rivalry with [Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_national_football_team) was once considered the greatest football rivalry in the Middle East, until being taken over by Iraq's rivalry with Saudi Arabia due to Kuwait's decline. The rivalry began in the mid-1970s. Because of the Gulf War, Iraq and Kuwait were in complete avoidance and never met for more than 15 years until 2005. Iraq has played [44 matches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq%E2%80%93Kuwait_football_rivalry) against Kuwait with [19 victories, 13 draws, and 12 losses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq%E2%80%93Kuwait_football_rivalry).

### Cultural identity and supporters



Iraqi fans in London celebrating Iraq winning the [2007 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_AFC_Asian_Cup)

The Iraq national football team has frequently been viewed as a symbol of national unity within Iraqi society. During periods of war, sanctions and political instability, matches involving the national team often carried social and cultural significance beyond sport itself. Iraq's victory at the [2007 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_AFC_Asian_Cup) is widely regarded as one of the most significant moments in the country's sporting history. Celebrations following the victory took place across several Iraqi cities and among Iraqi communities abroad, with the triumph frequently described as a unifying national moment.

Iraqi supporter culture is characterised by patriotic chants and strong displays of national symbolism. Among the most widely used chants are "O Victorious Baghdad" ("منصورة يا بغداد") or "With our souls and our blood, we will redeem you, O Iraq" ("بالروح بالدم نفديك يا عراق") during the Iraqi team's matches.[^cite_note-113]

Another famous chant is "the first goal is coming" ("هسه يجي الاول") which is chanted in the beginning of the match.[^cite_note-114] A succeeding chant is "the second goal is coming" ("هسه يجي الثاني"); this is usually chanted repeatedly after Iraq score a goal to motivate the players to score another.

### Home matches in Iraq



[Al-Shaab Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Shaab_Stadium), Iraq's home ground from its opening in 1966 up until 2013

Iraq primarily plays its home matches at [Basra International Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basra_International_Stadium) but has also used various other venues across the country. Since 1980, FIFA has imposed bans on Iraq hosting competitive international matches on six occasions.

The first ban came in 1980 after fan and player violence during a match against [Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_national_football_team). Although lifted in 1982, the [Iran–Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Iraq_War) led to a renewed ban. During this period, Iraq played home games at neutral venues but still qualified for the [1986 FIFA World Cup](157231.md) and three [Olympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_Summer_Olympics) tournaments. The ban ended in 1988 after the war.[^cite_note-115]

A new ban followed the [Gulf War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War) in 1990 which lasted until 1995. Iraq hosted matches during the [1998](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(AFC\)) and [2002 FIFA World Cup qualifiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(AFC\)) but was again forced to play abroad following the [Iraq War](5043324.md) in 2003. Home matches resumed in 2009, but security concerns led to another ban in 2011.[^cite_note-AFC_green-light_to_Arbil_as_venue-116]

Between 2013 and 2018, Iraq hosted friendlies in [Baghdad](4492.md), [Basra](57580.md), and [Karbala](210957.md), culminating in FIFA lifting the ban in 2018. Basra hosted the [2018 AFC Cup Final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_AFC_Cup_Final),[^cite_note-117] while the [2019 WAFF Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_WAFF_Championship) was held in Karbala and [Erbil](24534207.md).[^cite_note-118] However, the [Iraqi protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%932021_Iraqi_protests) in 2019 led to another ban,[^cite_note-119] forcing Iraq to play its home games at neutral venues during the [2022 FIFA World Cup qualifiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(AFC\)).[^cite_note-120][^cite_note-121]

In [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/25th_Arabian_Gulf_Cup), Iraq successfully hosted the [Arabian Gulf Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabian_Gulf_Cup) for the first time since [1979](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5th_Arabian_Gulf_Cup),[^cite_note-122] and resumed hosting official matches for the [2026 FIFA World Cup qualifiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(AFC\)).[^cite_note-123]



[Basra International Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basra_International_Stadium), the primary venue for Iraq's home matches since its opening in 2013

| **First match:**  [Iraq](1039652.md) 2–1  [Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanon_national_football_team) – 23 May 2000, [King Abdullah II Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Abdullah_II_Stadium), [Amman](45007.md), Jordan  **Biggest win:**  [Iraq](1039652.md) 4–0  [Kyrgyzstan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyrgyzstan_national_football_team) – 27 May 2000, [King Abdullah II Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Abdullah_II_Stadium), [Amman](45007.md), Jordan  **Biggest defeat:**  [Iraq](1039652.md) 0–2  [Jordan](1090065.md) – 21 June 2004 and  [Iraq](1039652.md) 1–3  [Jordan](1090065.md) – 25 June 2004  **Best finish:** Champions (2002)  **Worst finish:** Group stage (2013)  **Overall top scorer:** [Razzaq Farhan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Razzaq_Farhan) (7 goals)  **Most goals in a single tournament:** [Razzaq Farhan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Razzaq_Farhan) (5 goals, 2000) |  |  |  |  |  |

| **First match:**  [Iraq](1039652.md) 1–0  [Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_national_football_team) – 13 November 1964, [Shuwaikh High School Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_University_Stadium), [Kuwait City](57021.md), Kuwait  **Biggest win:**  [Iraq](1039652.md) 10–1  [Bahrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahrain_national_football_team) – 5 April 1966, Al-Kashafa Stadium, [Baghdad](4492.md), Iraq  **Biggest defeat:**  [Iraq](1039652.md) 0–3  [Qatar](1111350.md) – 6 December 2021, [Al Bayt Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Bayt_Stadium), [Al Khor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Khor_\(city\)), Qatar  **Best finish:** Champions (1964, 1966, 1985, 1988)  **Worst finish:** Group stage (2021)  **Overall top scorer:** [Hisham Atta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hisham_Atta) (6 goals)  **Most goals in a single tournament:** [Nouri Dhiab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nouri_Dhiab) (5 goals, 1966)  *Draws include knockout matches decided via [penalty shoot-out](511302.md).* |  |  |  |  |  |

| **First match:**  [Iraq](1039652.md) 4–0  [Oman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oman_national_football_team) – 27 March 1976, [Khalifa Sports City Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalifa_International_Stadium), [Doha](26214389.md), Qatar  **Biggest win:**  [Iraq](1039652.md) 7–0  [Oman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oman_national_football_team) – 6 April 1979, [Al-Shaab Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Shaab_Stadium), [Baghdad](4492.md), Iraq  **Biggest defeat:**  [Iraq](1039652.md) 0–4  [Oman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oman_national_football_team) – 7 January 2009, [Sultan Qaboos Sports Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultan_Qaboos_Sports_Complex), [Muscat](57053.md), Oman  **Best finish:** Champions (1979, 1984, 1988, 2023)  **Worst finish:** Group stage (2004, 2007, 2009, 2014, 2024–25, 2026), Sixth place (1986)  **Overall top scorer:** [Hussein Saeed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hussein_Saeed) (22 goals)  **Most goals in a single tournament:** [Hussein Saeed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hussein_Saeed) (10 goals, 1979)  *Draws include knockout matches decided via [penalty shoot-out](511302.md).* |  |  |  |  |  |

Iraq's starting line-up against [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_national_football_team) in the [2007 AFC Asian Cup Final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_AFC_Asian_Cup_Final), a match they won 1–0.

### Continental

- **[AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Asian_Cup)**&#x20;

  - 1st place, gold medalist(s) **Champions (1)**: [2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_AFC_Asian_Cup)

- **[Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_Asian_Games)**&#x20;

  - 1st place, gold medalist(s) **Gold medal (1)**: [1982](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_1982_Asian_Games)

### Regional

- **[WAFF Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WAFF_Championship)**&#x20;

  - 1st place, gold medalist(s) **Champions (1)**: [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_WAFF_Championship)

  - 2nd place, silver medalist(s) Runners-up (3): [2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_WAFF_Championship), [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_WAFF_Championship), [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_WAFF_Championship)

  - 3rd place, bronze medalist(s) Third place (1): [2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_WAFF_Championship)

- **[Arab Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Arab_Cup)**&#x20;

  - 1st place, gold medalist(s) **Champions (4)**: [1964](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964_Arab_Cup), [1966](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1966_Arab_Cup), [1985](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1985_Arab_Cup), [1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Arab_Cup) **(record)**

  - 3rd place, bronze medalist(s) Third place (1): [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Arab_Cup)

- **[Arabian Gulf Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabian_Gulf_Cup)**&#x20;

  - 1st place, gold medalist(s) **Champions (4)**: [1979](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5th_Arabian_Gulf_Cup), [1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7th_Arabian_Gulf_Cup), [1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9th_Arabian_Gulf_Cup), [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/25th_Arabian_Gulf_Cup)

  - 2nd place, silver medalist(s) Runners-up (2): [1976](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4th_Arabian_Gulf_Cup), [2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/21st_Arabian_Gulf_Cup)

- **[West Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_West_Asian_Games)**&#x20;

  - 1st place, gold medalist(s) **Gold medal (1)**: [2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_2005_West_Asian_Games) **(joint record)**

- **[Arab Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_Arab_Games)**&#x20;

  - 1st place, gold medalist(s) **Gold medal (1)**: [1985](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_1985_Arab_Games)

  - 2nd place, silver medalist(s) Silver medal (1): [1999](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_1999_Arab_Games)
